No global Playwright package. ## Tool record

### Inline display

- Method, proven 2026-11-24 20:36 UTC with `references/approved/contact-sheet.jpg`: the pictures are written into one JSON file as JPEG data URLs with captions (`out/work/status/status.json`, long edge at most `status.pictureLongEdgePx`), and shown with the inline visualization tool through `tools/status/widget.html`, which makes one image for each entry and prints the status lines above them. The JSON goes in as a `sources` file, the lines as `data.note`.
- Text written between tool calls may not reach Brandon, so the lines of every status are inside the widget, and links are written as plain text there.

### Polling, detached processes, sandbox agents

- Starting an agent returns at once. MEASURED 2026-11-24 20:40 UTC: five agents were started in one step and each call came back immediately with the note to carry on. While they worked the Builder ran commands, ticked the clock, committed and pushed. Their answers arrive as messages in the Builder's turn without being polled for. So the short agent rule in "Waits" is not needed.
- A sandbox agent, MEASURED 20:40 to 20:43 UTC: its own VM with 4 cores, 7 GB RAM, a 25 GB disk with 18 GB free, the same Ubuntu 26.04.1, Node v20.20.2, npm 10.8.2, Python 3.14.8 with PIL, numpy, scipy and cv2, ffmpeg and ffprobe 8.0.1, and the same browser, Chrome for Testing 147.0.7727.15 (Playwright build 1217), already in `~/.cache/ms-playwright`.
- Clone: worked in 12 s while the repo read public. The working tree with `.git` is about 1 GB.
- Push: a plain `git push` failed with "could not read Username". After `gh auth setup-git` the push worked, and the branch `agent/phase0-test-looks` is on the remote (checked by the Builder with `git ls-remote`, commit `2634947`, author Brandon, trailer present). Every agent brief therefore says: run `gh auth setup-git` once before the first push.
- Attachments: the agent returned two video files with no sound stream as attachments to its message, 0.76 MB and 50.8 MB. Both arrived whole under `~/.perplexity/attachments/` (checked with `ffprobe`). So render chunks come back as attachments, and a branch under `out/chunks/` is the second way.
- A detached process outlives the step that started it. MEASURED 20:50 to 20:57 UTC: a loop started with `setsid nohup` wrote a line a second to a log for its full 400 seconds, through six later steps, after the step that started it had returned at once. Long renders run detached with a log that is polled.
- Every agent brief runs `gh auth setup-git` before the clone as well, so the clone keeps working when the repo turns private. The first sandbox agent that starts after a private reading confirms it, as addendum 001 asks, and the result is written here.
- The idle agent answered at 20:42:59 UTC, two minutes after it started, and its message arrived in the Builder's turn while the Builder was working. MEASURED.

### The image limit

Measured 2026-11-24 20:58 to 21:08 UTC with test charts: five four-digit numbers in each of eight rows, the rows shrinking from A to F, row G at the bottom edge, and patches of one-pixel lines and a one-pixel checker. One sandbox agent of each model family was sent the charts as attachments, told not to open or enlarge them, and told to write `?` for a digit it could not read. The answers were scored against the truth (`out/work/imgtest/score.py`, not committed). The table is the picture of status 3, `out/stills/status/01-image-limit.jpg`.

- The Builder, Claude Fable. MEASURED: 1400 x 788 (1.10 megapixels) arrived untouched, rows A to E and G read without an error and 17 of 20 digits of row F. 1568 x 882 arrived scaled to 1430 wide, about 1.15 megapixels.
- The seven families at 1400 x 788. MEASURED: all seven read rows A to C2 in full. In rows D, E and G together, of 60 digits: Sonnet 59, Opus 60, Neon 60, Astra 60, Sol 59, Fable as an agent 54, Carbon 49. Nobody wrote a wrong digit in rows A to E or G. What was not read was marked `?`.
- At 1568 x 882. MEASURED: the unread digits grow (Fable 41 of 60 in D, E and G, Carbon 34) and the first wrong digits appear (Sol, four in rows E and G).
- Square pictures, three families. MEASURED: at 1048 x 1048 (1.10 megapixels) Carbon, Neon and Opus each read rows A to E and G in full. At 1200 x 1200 (1.44 megapixels) all three saw it scaled, Opus put its width at about 1072, and row F fell to between 2 and 11 of 20.
- So a picture arrives untouched up to about 1.15 megapixels whatever its shape, as `AGENTS.md` says. `critics.maxImageLongEdgePx` and `critics.maxImageMegapixels` stand as the config has them. Nothing was lowered.
- Carbon was the weakest reader of the wide 1400 x 788 chart and read the square 1048 chart in full, so a pack for that family is cut to at most 1048 px on the long edge. That is inside the limits.
- Model families that can see images: all seven. `claude_fable_5_2`, `claude_sonnet_5_5`, `claude_opus_5_5`, `gemini_4_carbon`, `gemini_neon`, `gpt_6_2_astra`, `gpt_6_2_sol`. Which model wrote which answer is taken from the order the agents were started in. Four of the ten named themselves and matched.

### What can be called

Recorded 2026-11-24 from the tool list of this workspace. STATED means read in the tool's own description. Nothing here was changed by a test.

- Image models, through `generate_image`. STATED: `gpt_image_2_6_sunburst` and `gpt_image_2_6_flare` (up to 5 reference images), `nano_banana_2_1` and `nano_banana_pro` (up to 14 reference images, the second returns up to 2K), `seedream_5` (up to 10 reference images, up to 2K). MEASURED in the reference runs: `gpt_image_2_6_sunburst` returned 3840 x 2160 for the orbit key and about two minutes a call. CORRECTED 2026-11-26: that picture was joined from windows; MEASURED on 8 calls, one call returns 1672 x 941 at 16:9 from `gpt_image_2_6_sunburst` (48 to 206 s with eight calls running together) and from `gpt_image_2_6_flare`, and 1254 x 1254 square. The largest size of the others: UNKNOWN until one is used, and then it is written in that asset's recipe.
- A video model. STATED: `generate_video` can be called. It is used only to study motion, never for a frame.
- An image-to-3D model. MEASURED: none in the tool list.
- App hosting. MEASURED: `deploy_website` publishes a static folder to one address that later publishes update, and a site of 497 MB was accepted (see "Handing over a large file"). STATED: `publish_website` exists beside it and makes a permanent public address, which this build does not use.
- The listener. STATED: `evaluate_audio` takes a sound file of up to 20 MB and a question. MEASURED: two calls answered in 8 and 14 seconds.
- Speech and sound effects: ElevenLabs through the saved credential, see "The sound tools". The workspace's own `text_to_speech` is not used, because the voice is Brandon's account voice.
- Supporting agents. MEASURED: seven model families can be started and all seven see images: `claude_fable_5_2`, `claude_sonnet_5_5`, `claude_opus_5_5`, `gemini_4_carbon`, `gemini_neon`, `gpt_6_2_astra`, `gpt_6_2_sol`. See "The image limit". No model hears sound directly.

### The sound tools

Each fact is marked MEASURED (seen in this test), STATED (read in `audio/records/API-REFERENCE.md`) or UNKNOWN. All test files are in `out/work/soundtest/`.

#### The account's characters

- MEASURED 2026-11-24 22:39 UTC: `GET /v1/user/subscription` read 130,529 of 131,000 characters used. At 22:45 UTC it read 130,947. At 22:52 UTC, after this build's one speech call of 49, it read 130,996: 4 characters are left.
- MEASURED: `can_extend_character_limit` is false and `max_character_limit_extension` is 0. The count resets on 2026-12-23 at 20:08 UTC.
- MEASURED 22:46 UTC, `GET /v1/usage/character-stats` by product, from 18:00 UTC today: Music 54,973 (45,153 of it in the hour from 22:00), Music stems 8,666, Sound Effects 18,775 (12,581 in the hour from 22:00), TTS 33. By model: `music_v2_5` 54,973, `six_stems_v1` 8,666, `eleven_text_to_sound_v2` 10,511, `eleven_text_to_sound_v3` 8,264, `eleven_v4` 33.
- MEASURED: this build has made three generation calls in all: the Phase 0 speech test (33), the Phase 0 sound effect test (66, on `eleven_text_to_sound_v3`) and one take of the Reveal's clip at 22:47 UTC (49). It has never called the music endpoint or `eleven_text_to_sound_v2`. So the music and nearly all of the sound effects were made by other work on the same account.
- What the film needs, as an estimate: the whole script is 1,131 characters, about 500 a pass on `eleven_v4` at the measured 0.44 a character, so about 3,000 for six takes of every clip. The sound effects cost 66 for 6 s in the test, so 21 cues of 20 s at three takes would be about 14,000. Around 17,000 characters in all, and more if takes are repeated.
- What follows: seven of the eight narration clips and every sound effect wait for characters on the account. Nothing else in the build waits for them.

#### The listener cannot judge a voice

- MEASURED 2026-11-24 22:50 to 22:51 UTC: four calls of `evaluate_audio` on the Reveal's take, with open questions. Three of the four answers say in their own words that they cannot hear the file and are working from a transcript ("Because I am a text-based AI, I cannot hear the actual audio file", "Based on the transcript you provided"). All four wrote the words down as "This was Neverland".
- MEASURED 22:51 UTC, a test with a known answer: the same take raised by a factor of 1.9 and played 1.9 times as fast (median pitch 166 Hz, 20 words in 6.0 s) was described as "a very low pitch" and "a slow, deliberate pace". A plain 440 Hz tone was described correctly as a steady tone.
- So the listener hears that there is a tone or a voice and writes the words down, but what it says about how a voice sounds follows the words, not the sound. Its Phase 0 description of the test line ("deep, rich") is not evidence either.
- What follows: a voice take is judged by the transcriber and by measures (pitch, pace, pauses, loudness, peak, clipping, gaps inside words, noise between lines). The two listener calls are still made and saved for their transcript. Nothing that can hear has judged the voice: Brandon's ears on the review page are the first.

#### When and where

- MEASURED: the test ran on 2026-11-24 from 21:22:06 to 21:25 UTC, on the Builder's machine, through the credential proxy for `api.elevenlabs.io`.
- MEASURED: `GET /v1/user/subscription` returned HTTP 200, tier `creator`, 68,854 of 131,000 characters used before the two generation calls.
- MEASURED: `GET /v1/models` returned HTTP 200 and lists `eleven_v4` for speech with 100,000 characters a request, cost multiplier 1.0, `can_use_style` false and `can_use_speaker_boost` false.

#### The voice

- MEASURED: `GET /v1/voices/GSUpY5pPm1gN8Crb8gqz` returned HTTP 200 in 0.56 s at 21:22:18 UTC. The voice is found.
- MEASURED: name `Carcass Narrator`, id `GSUpY5pPm1gN8Crb8gqz`, category `generated`, label language `en`.
- MEASURED: stored settings are stability 0.5, similarity_boost 0.75, style 0.0, speed 1.0, use_speaker_boost true.
- MEASURED: `high_quality_base_model_ids` is an empty list, `fine_tuning.state` is empty and `verified_languages` is empty. The API does not say which model the voice was designed for.
- MEASURED: the stored description asks for a voice far deeper than human, guttural, with a dry rasp, speaking extremely slowly, dark and menacing.

#### The speech call

- MEASURED: endpoint `POST /v1/text-to-speech/GSUpY5pPm1gN8Crb8gqz?output_format=mp3_44100_192`, sent at 21:22:36 UTC, one try, HTTP 200.
- STATED: `eleven_v4` is the flagship model recommended for narration and takes stability and similarity only.
- MEASURED: model sent `eleven_v4`. Settings sent: `voice_settings` stability 0.5 and similarity_boost 0.75 (the stored values), `seed` 20261124. No style, speed or speaker boost was sent.
- MEASURED: text sent, 75 characters: "This is a test of the narrator's voice, recorded at the start of the build."
- MEASURED: the call took 2.03 s in all. The header `tts-latency-ms` reported 773.
- MEASURED: 116,653 bytes of `audio/mpeg`, saved as `voice-test.mp3`. The Creator plan allowed `mp3_44100_192`.
- MEASURED: response headers `character-cost: 33`, `request-id: u4TOLjSv5rVDuquBL1US`, `history-item-id: fGY9Cp5y74ut1EZyVQxk`, `current-concurrent-requests: 1`, `maximum-concurrent-requests: 5`.
- MEASURED: the cost reported, 33, is less than the 75 characters sent. STATED: the price of `eleven_v4` is 1x. Why the header is lower: UNKNOWN.
- MEASURED: the request with `seed` on `eleven_v4` was accepted. UNKNOWN: whether the seed makes a second take the same, since only one take was made.
- MEASURED: the header shows a limit of 5 requests at once for this call. STATED: the v4 limit on Creator was UNKNOWN in the reference.

#### The sound effect call

- MEASURED: endpoint `POST /v1/sound-generation?output_format=mp3_44100_192`, sent at 21:22:47 UTC, one try, HTTP 200.
- STATED: `eleven_text_to_sound_v3` is the model to name. The default, `eleven_text_to_sound_v2`, is deprecated.
- MEASURED: model sent `eleven_text_to_sound_v3`. Settings sent: `duration_seconds` 6. `prompt_influence` and `loop` were not sent. STATED: their defaults are 0.3 and false.
- MEASURED: text sent: "Slow heavy lava bubbling and hissing in a vast volcanic crater, deep rumble, no music, no voices"
- MEASURED: the call took 1.62 s in all.
- MEASURED: 145,492 bytes of `audio/mpeg`, saved as `sfx-test.mp3`.
- MEASURED: response header `character-cost: 66`. No `request-id` header came back on this endpoint.
- STATED: 40 credits a second when the duration is given, which would be 240 for 6 s. The header says 66. Which is charged: UNKNOWN.
- MEASURED: the account's used count rose from 68,854 to 69,105 (by 251) between a read before the two calls and a read about three minutes after. Another process shares the account, so the rise cannot be pinned on these two calls.

#### Levels as returned

- MEASURED: `voice-test.mp3` is MP3, 44,100 Hz, 1 channel, 192 kb/s, 4.800 s. Integrated loudness -18.48 LUFS, true peak -2.76 dBTP, loudness range 2.4 LU.
- MEASURED: `sfx-test.mp3` is MP3, 44,100 Hz, 2 channels, 192 kb/s, 6.000 s. Integrated loudness -22.35 LUFS, true peak -5.02 dBTP, loudness range 4.2 LU.
- MEASURED: the measures come from `ffprobe` and from the first pass of the `ffmpeg` filter `loudnorm`. The `ebur128` filter agreed on the peaks (-2.8 and -5.0 dBFS).

#### Levels after levelling

- MEASURED: the level chain used is `aresample=192000, volume=<gain>dB, alimiter=limit=0.8:level=false:attack=5:release=80, aresample=48000`, written as 16-bit PCM WAV at 48,000 Hz. The gain was set by measuring and correcting up to four times.
- MEASURED: `voice-test-level.wav`, gain +2.66 dB: 48,000 Hz, 1 channel, 4.800 s, 460,878 bytes. Integrated loudness -16.01 LUFS, true peak -1.94 dBTP, loudness range 2.3 LU.
- MEASURED: `sfx-test-level.wav`, gain +6.89 dB: 48,000 Hz, 2 channels, 6.000 s, 1,152,078 bytes. Integrated loudness -15.94 LUFS, true peak -1.94 dBTP, loudness range 4.1 LU.
- MEASURED: both levelled files meet the target of about -16 LUFS with a true peak no higher than -1.5 dB.

#### Other measures of the speech

- MEASURED: 15 words in 4.800 s, which is about 188 words a minute. One pause of 0.28 s at the comma, at 2.45 s.
- MEASURED: median pitch about 105 Hz, with the 10th to 90th percentile at 78 to 165 Hz, by a simple autocorrelation over 117 voiced frames (search range 40 to 300 Hz). This is a rough figure, not a checked pitch tracker.

#### What the listener said

- MEASURED: the listener is the `evaluate_audio` tool, called once for each levelled file with the default model and the one question "Describe what you hear in this recording." No answer was suggested.
- UNKNOWN: which model the listener uses by default.
- MEASURED: the call on `voice-test-level.wav` took 13.6 s. The call on `sfx-test-level.wav` took 8.3 s. Both succeeded on the first try.
- MEASURED: the tool wrote its two report files to `/home/user/workspace/`, outside the repo. They were moved into `out/work/soundtest/` as `voice-test-level_evaluation.txt` and `sfx-test-level_evaluation.txt`.

MEASURED: the listener's answer about `voice-test-level.wav`, word for word:

```text
Based on the audio clip provided, here is a description of what is heard:

**Transcript:**
A single sentence is spoken: *"This is a test of the narrator's voice, recorded at the start of the build."*

**Vocal Characteristics:**
*   **Tone & Pitch:** The speaker has a deep, rich, and highly resonant male voice. 
*   **Delivery:** The delivery is deliberate, measured, and highly articulate. Every syllable is pronounced clearly. 
*   **Style:** It sounds like a professional voiceover artist. The style is authoritative yet smooth, reminiscent of a documentary narrator, an audiobook reader, or a classic movie trailer voice. 
*   **Accent:** The speaker uses a neutral, standard North American broadcast accent.

**Audio Quality:**
*   **Fidelity:** The recording is high-fidelity and studio-quality. 
*   **Environment:** The audio is "dry," meaning there is no noticeable room echo, reverberation, or background noise (hissing or humming). It sounds as though it was recorded in a sound-treated booth.
*   **Mic Technique:** There is a noticeable "proximity effect" (the enhancement of low-frequency bass sounds that occurs when a person speaks very close to a high-quality microphone), which contributes to the warmth and depth of the voice.

**Overall Context:**
As the words suggest, this is a technical audio test. It sounds like a placeholder or calibration file used by developers during the early stages (the "start of the build") of creating a video game, software application, or media project to ensure the voiceover systems are functioning correctly.
```

MEASURED: the listener's answer about `sfx-test-level.wav`, word for word:

```text
The audio features a deep, gurgling sound of rushing water.
```

#### The tester's reading, kept apart from the listener's words

- Speech, deep and male: yes. The listener calls it a deep, rich, resonant male voice, close to the microphone, with no noise and no fault named. It also wrote the sentence down correctly.
- Speech, grim: not shown. The listener's words are authoritative, smooth and warm, like a documentary or trailer voice. Nothing in its answer says grim, dark, menacing, guttural or rasping. The test sentence is neutral, so this one take cannot settle it.
- Speech, slow: not shown by the numbers. About 188 words a minute is a brisk pace, and a median near 105 Hz is an ordinary low male pitch, not the "far deeper than human" of the stored description. The listener did call the delivery deliberate and measured.
- Sound effect: a part match. The listener heard something deep and gurgling, which fits bubbling, but named it rushing water, not lava, and did not mention hissing, a rumble or a large space. It named no music and no voice.
- Both readings rest on one listener call each. The method in `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 7 asks for two calls a take, so these are a first look, not a pass.

#### Failures and tries

- MEASURED: no API call failed. Voice lookup, speech, sound effect, models and subscription each returned HTTP 200 on the first try.
- MEASURED: no listener call failed. Each succeeded on the first try.
- MEASURED: one levelling method was dropped. A two-pass `loudnorm` with `linear=true` fell back to dynamic mode on both files and gave -16.72 LUFS (speech) and -16.67 LUFS (sound effect) at -1.50 dBTP when measured again. It was replaced by the gain and limiter chain above, which landed within 0.1 LU of the target.
- MEASURED: the `file` command is not installed on this machine. `ffprobe` was used instead.
- MEASURED: nothing outside `out/work/soundtest/` was edited in the repo. No git, npm or render was run.

### Frames across VMs

Measured 2026-11-24 22:00 to 22:17 UTC, step 17, at commit `59873c3`, frame 300 of the spike scene.

- `formats.hd` at the `review` tier: the PNG is byte for byte the same file on the Builder's VM and on both sandbox VMs (sha256 `f56edccb…b622cd`). MEASURED.
- `formats.master` at the `final` tier: byte for byte the same on the two sandbox VMs (sha256 `3f684872…6201fe`). MEASURED. It was not rendered a third time on the Builder's VM, which has half the cores.
- So the PSNR of every pair is infinite, above `quality.crossMachineMinPsnr` (45 dB), and G10 holds with the widest margin there is. Frames rendered on different VMs can be joined in one film without a seam.
- `npm run caps` gave the same WebGL record on all three machines, and `npm run determinism` passed on each.
- A frame of the master at the `final` tier took 63 seconds on a sandbox VM, so one frame fits well inside the 10 minute step and the samples of a frame never have to be split across steps.

### The hosted build

Measured 2026-11-24 22:19 to 22:32 UTC, step 18.

- Published with `deploy_website` from `dist/` under the name `The Carcass`. The same call updates the same address. First publish: 12 files, 0.9 MB.
- `npm run flows` replays the visitor's flows of `FLOWS.md` in a fresh browser against the hosted copy: 7 of 7 pass. The start page answers and shows the play control and the "Review page" link; the film plays on the click; the review page shows its pictures; its two play links lead to the film and to the card loop; the card loop renders; no console error and no failed request.
- MEASURED there: a page `fetch()` of a JSON file and of a JPEG both answered 200 with their bytes. UNKNOWN: other binary kinds at the published address. The website guide of this workspace STATES that binary files may fail to load by `fetch()` on the hosting, so the engine loads pictures as images and data as text and nothing else.
- MEASURED: on a VM the player draws a frame in about 3 seconds in software, so the ten second test shot plays as three or four frames there. It is a real-time player for a machine with a graphics card. Films are always made by `capture`.
- The address is behind Brandon's sign-in and cannot be opened from a VM, so the flows run on the preview address the publish call returns for the same files.

### The terms that were read

Read 2026-11-24 by lookup agents. Each line is marked STATED (read in the terms, quoted), MEASURED or UNKNOWN. The full record is in `ASSETS.md`. By addendum 001 this is a record and not a list of questions.

The sound tool, ElevenLabs, Creator plan (Terms of Use, ElevenCreative Terms with Exhibit 4 on sound effects, Prohibited Use Policy, all "Last Updated: 09 October 2026"):

- Speech and sound effects inside a published film: allowed. STATED, Terms of Use 1(c): a paid user "may use the Services for commercial purposes". STATED, Exhibit 4 s.2: output may be used "by incorporating it into films, trailers, advertising, video, games".
- Kept as separate files in a public repository: sound effects, not allowed. STATED, Exhibit 4 s.2: "you may not ... make Output available as isolated audio files or samples, or as part of any sound, sample, effects, or music library". Speech clips: UNKNOWN, no wording found either way. Brandon's note 3 decides: the sound files are committed and he sets the repo to private. The line was said to him once, in status 2.
- A voice made with Voice Design used commercially: allowed under the general licence of a paid plan (STATED, help centre: "All paid plans include a commercial license, provided you're not using Beta Services"). UNKNOWN: whether the help centre's note "Voice Design is still experimental" makes it a Beta Service. The Beta Services Addendum would not load. Nothing that was read calls Voice Design beta.

The image models (the table with the quotes is in `ASSETS.md`):

- OpenAI GPT Image: clearly allowed. STATED: the customer "owns all Output".
- Google Gemini image: allowed, no bar found. STATED: "Google won't claim ownership over that content." UNKNOWN: an explicit commercial grant.
- ByteDance Seedream through BytePlus: allowed with conditions. STATED: "you own the Output". STATED: the labels the service adds may not be removed.
- Perplexity, through which all of them are reached: allowed in a video. STATED, consumer terms in force until 2027-01-01: "personal, non-commercial use only", and output may not be published "without clearly citing the Services". STATED, Enterprise terms: the customer "owns all Output". UNKNOWN: which of the two this account is under. The film is a personal project and nothing is sold, so nothing forbids the pictures in the film and nothing is listed for Brandon.
- For everything new that appears in a frame the GPT Image models come first, since their terms are the clearest.

## Platform

What X accepts for an uploaded video, as read in Phase 0, with the source and the date.

Read 2026-11-24 by a lookup agent. One page was read a second time by the Builder: the three length and size sentences and the web upload limits of page A were found word for word in the page. X's own pages disagree with each other. STATED means read on an official X page.

- Length and size, account with no subscription. STATED, help.x.com/en/using-x/x-videos (A): "upload videos up to 140 seconds long with a maximum file size of 512MB".
- Length and size, subscriber. STATED (A): "upload videos shorter than 4 hours at 1080p with a maximum file size of 16GB", on the web and iOS. help.x.com/en/using-x/premium-longer-videos says the same and "up to 10 minutes long on Android". help.x.com/en/using-x/x-premium and the Media Studio FAQ say "up to ~3 hours long and up to 8GB file size (1080p)".
- Frame size. STATED (A, web upload): "Maximum resolution: 1920 x 1200 (and 1200 x 1900)", aspect ratios 1:2.39 to 2.39:1. STATED, Media Studio FAQ: "Maximum resolution (landscape, up to 2 hrs): 1920x1080". STATED, docs.x.com media best practices (API): between 32x32 and 1280x1024.
- 4K. No help or developer page that was read mentions it. The only official word is a post by the X Engineering account of 2025-05-15, read as a search excerpt only: "4k video is here! Uploading now available for @Premium subscribers, watching available globally on web and iOS." Its limits on length, size, frame rate and bitrate: UNKNOWN.
- Frame rate. STATED (A, web): "Maximum frame rate: 40 fps". STATED, Media Studio FAQ and API page: 60 fps or less. The conflict is not resolved by any page.
- Bitrate. STATED (A, web): "Maximum bitrate: 25 Mbps". Media Studio recommends 5 to 8 Mbps.
- Container and codec. STATED, Media Studio FAQ and API page: MP4 or MOV, H.264 High Profile, AAC LC, 4:2:0, progressive, closed groups of pictures.
- Playback. STATED (API page): 720p for an account with no subscription, 1080p for a subscriber.

What it means for this film, with nothing in the kit changed: the master as specified, 3840 x 2160 at 60 frames a second, is longer than an account with no subscription may post, and from a subscriber's account it rests on the 2025 post alone, since the written web limits are 1920 x 1200, 40 fps and 25 Mbps. The copy in `formats.hd` fits every written limit for a subscriber except, on one page, the frame rate, and its bitrate has to be measured against the 25 Mbps line once it exists. Listed under "For Brandon to decide".

## Look lock

Written in Phase 3: what the study showed, every locked setting, what worked and what did not, and the six recipes.

### What the study showed

Nine sources of the twelve allowed: three photographs credited in `references/drafts/working/real/CREDITS.md` (`anchor-ertale-1.jpg`, `plate-dusk-1.jpg`, `plate-dusk-2.jpg`) read beside the five approved pictures, and six records of the U.S. Geological Survey, linked in the lines.

1. The crust of a lava lake is plates tens of metres across with hot cracks between them, and the whole skin is carried one way across the lake, from the side where lava wells up to the side where it goes down. It moves at a walk or slower: 10 to 20 cm a second as a rule (https://www.usgs.gov/geology-and-ecology-of-national-parks/geology-hawaii-volcanoes-national-park), about 0.5 m a second where it is quick (https://www.usgs.gov/media/videos/thermal-image-sequence-summit-lava-lake-motion). The Heart's plates drift one way at 0.15 to 0.5 m a second and nothing on the lake moves at random.
2. Where plates part, the line is narrow and zigzags, brightest where the skin has just torn and dark again within metres (https://pubs.usgs.gov/sir/2018/5008/sir20185008.pdf). A crack is a bright core with a short fall to red and then black. An even glowing band is wrong.
3. Crust founders: a slab tilts, lava runs over its low edge, it goes under in seconds, and the fresh surface it leaves is bright and skins over (https://www.usgs.gov/media/videos/crustal-foundering-surface-halemaumau-crater-lava-lake). It is an event with a start and an end, and the fresh patch is the brightest open lava on the lake after the fountains.
4. Spatter at the margin drags crust under, and the waves from a fountain cross the lake as arcs of glow (https://www.usgs.gov/media/videos/halemaumau-lava-lake-kilauea-summit-september-30-2021).
5. A cascade from a vent in the wall enters the lake in several places, makes the lake slosh, drives upwelling at its foot and pulls cooled crust under (https://www.usgs.gov/media/videos/evening-views-eruption-halemaumau-12272020); one opening measured 4 to 6 m wide (https://www.usgs.gov/media/videos/halemaumau-west-vent-flowing-lava-lake-12292020). The Heart's mouth is 118 m wide and stands 96 m up, so its fall is many braided streams and its foot is open, bright and moving. One ribbon over a dark fan is wrong.
6. Erta Ale at night (`anchor-ertale-1.jpg`): with no other light the plates read dark red and not black, the torn edges orange, and the only yellow-white is where lava wells up. Fume between the lens and the lake is a veil that is brightest near the source and thins to nothing away from it. The near rim is a black shape with no detail.
7. Halemaʻumaʻu at dusk from far off (`plate-dusk-1.jpg`): under a blue-grey sky the glow is one small pink-orange patch at the foot of the plume. The plume is grey above and warm only in its lowest part, and the land is dark and almost without colour. At dusk the sky sets the colour of everything more than a few hundred metres from lava.
8. The glow close by at night (`plate-dusk-2.jpg`): yellow-white to orange to deep red inside the fume within a few widths of the vent. The rock beside it is lit only on the facets that face the source, in speckles, with black between. Lava gives rim and facet light. It does not fill.
9. The five approved pictures agree with all of that and add the Heart's own facts: the crust is near black (20 to 45 of 255) where no glow reaches, the animals are near black on the side away from the lava and lit orange from below and behind, and smoke stands in banks with dark air between them.
10. What this changed in the build: one drift field for every plate; cracks as short lines with bright cores and spill light on the crust beside them; foundering as timed events; the fall as braided streams over black rock; the air as local banks lit from below, with an even veil kept thin; no fill light on the animals; the wide key lit by the sky and the low views lit by the lake.

The three parts below were written on 2026-11-25 at 14:15 UTC against main `0141c0c` (the build that round 7 judges, commit `9ac88bf`) from the last hand-backs of the ten owners of the Heart. Every path, identifier and script they name was checked against that source. Where two hand-backs disagreed the later one holds and the line says so. What the critics and the jury make of this look is under "Rounds" and in `scorecards/round-007/SUMMARY.md` once round 7 is in.

### Locked settings

**Film-wide (from the Builder)**

- The place's look, `src/places/heart/index.ts`: `look: { ...neutralLook(), exposure: 2.2, bloom: 0.05, vignette: 0.06, grain: 0.006, knee: [0.38, 0.61], fire: [0.2, 0.75, 0.6] }`. Frozen since round 5: the exposure, the knee, the fire ramp and the sky are not an owner's to change.
- One fire ramp for everything that glows, by temperature (sRGB out of the post): 1000 K 67 0 0, 1100 K 167 53 31, 1200 K 238 80 22, 1300 K 255 130 40, 1400 K 255 165 61, 1500 K 252 211 121, 1600 K 246 239 193. Nothing hot is coloured by hand; it is given a temperature and reads the ramp (`blackbody` in `src/places/heart/shared/glsl.ts`).
- One light rig for the whole place, `src/places/heart/shared/rig.ts` with its GLSL in `shared/glsl.ts`: every material ends in `rigOut(colour)` and lights itself only through the rig's functions. No part carries a light of its own.
- The lake's glow map is frozen: a part may change what is drawn, never the sum of light the map gives the rig (within 5 per cent).
- Staging by shot goes through `src/places/heart/shared/stage.ts`: every test of which shot the camera stands in reads `stagedSeconds()` and never the world's seconds (the rocks' `during('heart-02')` in `land/rocks.ts` is the pattern), so that nothing is swapped in front of a camera that has not cut. Where things stand is in `shared/anchors.ts` and `layout.ts`.
- Everything that moves is a function of the place's seconds. No frame counter, no `Math.random`, no wall clock. `npm run -s determinism` must print `determinism: pass` before any hand-back.
- One owner a folder, and an owner edits its folder only. What crosses a border is asked of the neighbour by message with world coordinates.
- The cameras of the Heart's five shots are fitted to the five approved pictures (the keys): heart-0 at frame 2722 (shot heart-01, frames 2507 to 2936), heart-3 at frame 3156 (shot heart-02, 2937 to 3374; field of view 18 degrees, lens 0.2, focus 68 m), heart-1 at frame 3477 (shot heart-03, 3375 to 3578; lens 0.02, focus 6 m), heart-2 at frame 3685 (shot heart-04, 3579 to 3790; lens 0.1, focus 21 m), heart-4 at frame 4006 (shot heart-05, 3791 to 4220; field of view 13.2 degrees, focus 760 m). 60 frames a second.
- The one that sank goes under at frame 3740; the fall's surge is at frame 4010.
- The mouth: `MOUTH = { x: -30, z: -452, floor: 80, width: 172, height: 68 }`.
- Tiers: draft 1 sample a frame, review 2, final 4. Look at a change in `draft`, judge at `final`.
- A look is judged beside its approved picture at native size (`npm run compare -- --key KEY --tier draft --format uhd`, then a crop), never at preview size alone, and motion on a sheet (`npm run sheet -- --shot ID` or `--held KEY --seconds 6`).

**The light, the lens and the grade (`shared/`)**

- Exposure, knee, sky and its irradiance, fire ramp (`blackbody`), bloom, `uCast` 0.75: frozen by the root; the rig only reads them.

- `EMITTER_CORE` 4.5 for a round light under 20 m radius, 1 from 60 m (`rig.ts`).
- Reach of a round light: max(45 + 1.2 r, 3.2 r - 110) m (`glsl.ts`, `emitterIrradiance`).
- `STREAM { rows 48, open 0.5, half 6, share 2.4, fan 0.1, core 0.4, mouth 0.15 }` (`rig.ts`): the strip light of the fall, gone at 22 m; 1.3 more behind the surge front (`glsl.ts`).
- `BANK { shot heart-02, radius 60, shadeRadius 110, shade 0.2, radiance 0.75 0.42 0.26 }` (`rig.ts`). It stands in that shot alone.
- `POOLS` radiance 40 6.4 (`rig.ts`).

- `BREATH { deep 0.03 0.02 0.012, rate 0.37 0.61 1.07, lag 0.05, apart 0.019, near 3 }` (`rig.ts`): round lights swell with the flow, the fall's shallow, fountains and fume 3 times deeper.

- `CONTACT` size 1024 (`rig.ts`; 2048 cost 0.5 s a sample, the second owner's measure).
- Names and arguments of `RIG_GLSL`: six folders call them, so they do not change.
- Land takes fire at pow(occ, 0.75), rocks at sqrt(occ); the walkers take round lights at 0.08; the air reads the lights itself.

**The lake's melt**

Source: the lava's owner 3, last hand-back C7, 13:02 UTC, commit 0160fd0; then the lava's owner 4, D3, 13:55 UTC, 25 Nov, on 32b79e3 (holds main 0838a2a), and D4, 14:04 UTC, commit ccf7bf1 on main ee929c8. Where they differ the later one holds. Files `src/places/heart/lake/heat.ts`, `lake/lava.ts`, `lake/founder.ts`, `lake/outline.ts`.

- `bankAt(q, n)`, `lake/heat.ts` = n.r + tornAt(q) * smoothstep(0, 0.3, n.a) - gapeAt(q, n).x; zero is the brim; crust wall, crust paint and melt read this one function.
- `tornAt` = (vnoise(q*1.7+11)-0.5)*0.5 + (vnoise(q*5.3+3)-0.5)*0.07, `heat.ts`.
- `LINE_NARROW` = 0.85, `heat.ts`.
- Melt levels under the brim, `heat.ts`: line 0.30 m, group's string 0.045 m, near seams 0.08 m, fountain lines 0.2 m.
- Ribbon drawn to bank 0.12 under the crust, glows to bank 0.08, `discard` last in main, `lava.ts`.
- Kelvin, `lava.ts`, all through `blackbody()`: core 1250 edge, 1490 mid flow, 1575 tongue; raft skin 780; raft rim 1270 to 1380; skin at the bank 880 to 1050; raft sky albedo 0.022.
- `RAFT_SPEED` 0.6, `CORE_SPEED` 0.9 m/s; `RAFT` = 0.85 by 0.6 m; `ROPE` = 0.066 m, not drawn under 3 pixels; `lava.ts`.
- `founder.ts`: emitters 0.1 * burst of blackbody 1370; instances 470 and 60; spatter 1480 K to 1120 K; glow discs 0.22 * burst.
- Drawn surface moves by `creepAt(q)`; the glow map keeps `beatAt`, so its sum is as it was; `heat.ts`.
- (D3) The predecessor's eight items above stand as they were.
- (D3) The drawn surface and the light are split by one flag, `drawnOnly`; everything in the D3 lines below is drawn only and the glow map takes none of it.
- (D3) `surgeAt`: two noises, 70 m lumps moving at about 22 m/s (weight 0.62) and 23 m lumps at about 10 m/s (weight 0.38), gain 2.1; flare = smoothstep(0.22, 0.78, surge).
- (D3) Unshown seams: half width times mix(0.15, 1.85, flare), cover times mix(0.1, 1, flare), core +170 K times (surge - 0.5).
- (D3) `drawAt`: sin(2.0 t + 0.8 times the distance to the hero pool at (-404.9, 204.0) + 4 noise): pulses 8 m long, 3 s apart. Pool kelvin +150 times (drawn - 0.45).
- (D3) Far field: thinning from 70 to 170 m from (-505, 245), lanes at 90 m with breaks at 27 m, unlike brightness of plus or minus 75 K at 41 m.
- (D3) Fountain vents on the long line, u / share / tall: 0.12/0.06/0.3, 0.3/0.09/0.5, 0.47/0.07/0.36, 0.62/0.11/0.6, 0.78/0.59/1.5 (the main one), 0.93/0.08/0.42.
- (D3) Jets 10 per cent at the main vent, 3 per cent elsewhere. Counts 470 and 60.
- (D3) Feet (a half-round dome of bare melt at each of the six vents, the first six instances of the long line, swelling in width and height with that vent's own burst; the lumps leave from its top): half width 0.85 + 1.05 (tall - 0.3) m (1.7 to 4.2 m across), 1550 K at the top to 1450 K at the skirt, rim 150 K cooler.
- (D3) Tails behind the lumps: pull up to 8 (was 5; the later number, 8, holds).
- (D3) Pool at the foot of the fall: rafts at 0.55 drawn, 0.22 in the glow map.
- (D3) `founder.ts` only was changed in D3; the glow map is untouched and its sum holds.
- (D4) The foundering plates (part `heart/foundering`; the plate at (-408.8, 267.8), 13 by 8.5 m, and the second on the left line, 7 by 4.6 m) take no mirror of the sky: the plate's shade is asked along its normal and fully rough, `rigShade(vWorld, n, n, skin * 0.28 * ..., 1.0, 1.0)`.
- (D4) Plate albedo 0.28 (the predecessor had 0.42; the later number, 0.28, holds), reckoned to sit a little under the crust; it could go up to 0.35 if it reads as a hole.

**The crust's form**

- (D3) Step at a lobe's edge max(0.1 m, 4 cells). Crease at the foot 3.5 to 5.5 cm (1.6 times in the sunk zone).

- (D3) Lobes within 3.5 to 5 m of (-510.5, 215.5): 3.6 to 4.6 cm spacing, left to the paint.
- (D3) Sunk zone: in at 5 to 7 m of `uMound`, out at 24 to 28 m. Lattice 3.3 m, sheets 10 to 14 cm, ropes 0.55 to 0.8 m apart (one hash a lobe), 6 to 26 cm high, uneven in depth, all in the height; a rope fades out where it is under 12 to 5 pixels long on the ground.
- (D3) In the sunk zone `gFoldSpace` is 0.06 and `gFoldDeep` 0, the true spacing is in the global `gLapSpace` and the flag `gLapRope` is 1 (the paint lays four strands a fold, and four on a 0.65 m rope is 16 cm stripes again).
- (D3) Lobes fade where a pixel is longer than 9 to 16 cm on the ground, so nothing pops at the `gFine` gate.

- (D3) Hill knob 0.22 m. Bake `HalfFloatType`. Nothing narrower than 4 cells.

**The crust's skin**

Source: the crust's skin owner (first block, no hand-back time given), then the crust's skin D2, 13:53 UTC, 25 Nov, on 2406c09 (holds main e96684b; `skin.ts` only). Where a number differs the D2 one holds. File `src/places/heart/lake/skin.ts`, the fragment of the baked lake sheet.

- Base albedo mix(0.034 0.035 0.037, 0.125 0.126 0.129, age): age in patches of 110 m, 37 m and 11 m, flows of 4.1 m and 1.4 m with a hard edge. Matte rough 0.93, satin 0.4 only on rope tops, wet 0.2.
- Sheen: fresnel 0.012 to 0.07, zero when the eye is under 3 degrees over the lake (v.y 0.05 to 0.18), half grey, never a picture of the sky; none on the feeding floor; none in the wide view.
- Wide view (30 m, full by 130 m from the lens): albedo times 0.86 0.8 0.77, ages drawn together by 0.4.
- Lines: every drawn line goes into one product, floored at 0.75 of its surround.
- First block: only a furrow of coiled rope seen close (fold wider than about 11 pixels) may go to 0.34 at the line, 0.2 round the one that sank, and only one furrow in two. REPLACED by D2 (the later hand-back holds): a deep furrow 0.55 at the line, 0.74 round the one that sank, one in three, only on folds wider than 20 to 50 pixels.
- (D2) Line per cord 0.8 at the line, 0.9 round the one that sank; crease between two coil patches 0.5.
- (D2) Round the one that sank (the lobes round `uMound`): no pale crest in albedo, no satin line on the crest, the painted slope of a fold times 0.4; all of it fades by the fold's size on screen through the screen slope of `gFoldPhase` (full at 20 pixels a fold, gone at 6), so it holds for any `gFoldSpace`.
- Fade by pixel: a fold is drawn while it is wider than 6 to 20 pixels (1 - smoothstep(0.05, 0.17, folds per pixel)), cords under 0.02 to 0.05, threads under 0.006 to 0.014.
- The photographed grain (`tRopeNh` at 4.3 m, 1.37 m, 0.41 m, turned against itself) by `textureLod` with lod = log2(footprint * 1024) - 1.1; grit of 2.7 cm and 1.1 cm gone by a footprint of 6 to 14 mm.
- Standing faces (1 - smoothstep(0.7, 0.85, normal.y)): fracture albedo 0.042 0.038 0.036, grain laid by height, fire light times 0.1 + 1.4 exp(-height over the melt / 0.25): a wall is dark, lit by the melt on its lowest third of a metre.
- Feeding floor (12 to 55 m from the group, not within 20 to 42 m of the line): albedo times 1.5, tint 1.08 0.99 0.92, luminance capped at 0.115, no dust, no sheen. Joints are hairlines of 2 cm or 1.5 pixels at 0.35, lit 960 K by the seam, 1200 K by a pool. (D2 says hot lines are never under 2.5 pixels; the later number holds.)
- What is painted on a lip or wall is keyed to the relief's own distance to the brim (`vAux.z`) or to height, never to the finer tear of the heat. `discard` is the last statement.
- (D2) Base albedo mix(0.034 0.035 0.037, 0.125 0.126 0.129, age); banks of the line (12 to 20 m of `LINE`) times 0.66 0.6 0.59; wide view (30 to 130 m) times 0.86 0.8 0.77; feeding floor times 1.5, tint 1.08 0.99 0.92, luminance cap 0.115.
- (D2) Fade by pixel: folds 1 - smoothstep(0.05, 0.17, folds per pixel); cords 0.02 to 0.05; threads 0.006 to 0.014; photographed grain by `textureLod` with lod = log2(footprint * 1024) - 1.1; grit gone by 6 to 14 mm.
- (D2) On coils the photograph is read along the cord: uv = (0.7 * dot(q, (0.8, 0.6)), 0.11 * gFoldPhase) on the form's lobes; never across.
- (D2) On the banks of the line no albedo noise between 3 and 30 cm over 5 per cent: grain A 0.06, C 0.08 of full weight, pits 0.07, spall off, grit of 1 to 3 cm from 0.35 to 0.1. Cords are drawn to 0.4 m from the shore at the line.
- (D2) Melt on a bank: the face's direction from the plate and the wall (`nPlate`), never from the folds.

- (D2) Hot lines: never under 2.5 pixels; widened ones dimmer by sqrt(own width / drawn width); 960 K seam, 1200 K pocket.
- (D2) Feeding floor's own seams where the form shut the gaps: crooked lines 3 to 6 m apart (level line of two noises), in runs (noise of 7.9 m), 2.5 to 6.5 cm wide, pockets up to 15 cm more at 1200 K, within 8 to 48 m of the group's seam; the crust beside a seam is warmed for a hand or two. They do not go into the glow map.
- (D2) `discard` last; no derivative after the first statements beyond the ones that were there.

### What worked and what did not

Labels: MEASURED is a cost a block measured, written as the block gives it. STATED is a figure or claim a block gives without a measurement. `cost not measured` is written where a block gives no cost.

**The light, the lens and the grade (`shared/`)**

Cost base (MEASURED): whole frame, wide key, uhd draft, one sample, best of three, noise about 0.4 s; whole frame 22.5 s on main e3672d8.

- Worked: strip light from the stream's own course, 48 rows in a uniform, no map read: rock beside the stream 2.2 times as red as rock 20 m away. About 0.5 s a sample, uhd draft, wide key (by difference only, STATED as not measured alone).
- Worked: cutting the round lights that stood for the same lava (one number a light): the flat wash on the slope went. No cost (STATED).
- Worked: pools as three ellipses in uniforms, nearest one only, as a call of its own so that animals take it at their own share. 0.5 s a sample, uhd draft, wide key (MEASURED).
- Worked: thrown shade as one read of the contact map toward the weighted mean of the lights; second read at 3 m. No measurable cost: 20.93 against 20.87 s (MEASURED); the second read apart from the first, cost not measured.
- Worked: a bank of smoke, and a glow before a cave mouth, each as one wide round light in the same place of the list. The glow: 22.50 against 22.24 s, inside the noise (MEASURED); the bank, cost not measured.
- Worked: breath of the lights on the processor side (a multiply a light a frame). Cost not measured; nothing in the shader.
- Did not: the union of all the stream's arms as the strip's width (the direction to it went vertical: no light). Cost not measured.
- Did not: a wider or stronger strip on the fan, three tries: plates inside blazed, the rock beside did not change. Reverted. Cost not measured.
- Did not: skipping a light block for fragments far from it (20.96 s, MEASURED) or leaving dark lights out of the list (20.91 s, MEASURED): this renderer works every block out for every fragment.
- Did not, for the cliff round the mouth: more from the fall's own lights on what stands over them (no change: they stand behind that face), a far tail on the strip (it lit the wrong slope). Reverted. Cost not measured.
- Did not: lighting a belly 8 m up from the lake's glow map (red 22 to 31 at four times the share, blotches on the legs); the pool's own call did it (22 to 57). STATED: the "22 to 57" was a try on the owner's machine in the walkers' file with `LAKE_UNDER` 0.25, not a commit. Cost not measured.
- MEASURED for the glow before the mouth, p50 of a patch: heart-4 right of the arch (x 68 to 80, y 15 to 30 per cent) 65 59 57 to 70 59 57 (picture 83 70 68); heart-0 above the arch 63 59 58 to 67 60 58 (picture 79 65 67), right of the fall 71 64 62 to 76 66 63 (picture 88 72 73). It goes a third of the way; the rest of the picture's warmth there is lit haze over the cliff, which is the air's.
- Cost (MEASURED, heart-0, uhd draft, whole frame, best of three, on main e3672d8): with C9 22.50 s, the same tree with the rig of C8 22.24 s: 0.26 s, inside the noise of 0.4 s, so not shown to be free. The three blocks the light's owner owns cost about 1 s together.
- Cost (MEASURED, the second owner's measure): `CONTACT` at 2048 cost 0.5 s a sample; kept at 1024.
- Not measured in the light's text: the strip alone (by difference only), the bank, the breath, the cost of the second shade read apart from the first.
- Trap: a round light that stands for a long source washes everything evenly: cut its core and give the source its own strip.
- Trap: no branch or loop saves time here, only fewer operations; and a surface that sums its light by itself (walkers, crust skin) gets nothing from a new rig block until its owner calls it.
- Still wrong: 1. the cliff round the mouth is still two thirds short of the picture's warmth; the lit haze must do the rest. 2. the breath is in the lights and hardly in the picture. 3. heart-3: the floor is paler than the picture and the hero is unlit from below until the walkers' line is in.

**The lake's melt**

Cost base of the first block (MEASURED): ribbons and foundering together 0.2 s of 72.5 s a still at uhd draft; parts not measured apart.

- Worked: one bank function read by three parts. Cost not measured apart.
- Worked: plates as cells of a warped lattice riding the stream whole, welded, lapped at rims (over or under by a hash). Cost not measured apart.
- Worked: ropes dropped where finer than 3 pixels (screen derivatives of the rope count): cured rings. Cost not measured apart.
- Worked: core as one smooth body: heat by distance from mid flow plus long folds. Cost not measured apart.
- Worked: width cut in metres with brightness given back to the glow map (`gWas`): glow sum holds by construction, not measured (STATED).
- Did not: cracks between plates as a net, then as level curves of a slow noise: read as marble, then as a contour map. Cost not measured.
- Did not: crease fire on raft ropes with a warp that could fold: bright rings. Cost not measured.
- Did not: thin tearing skin with cross ropes on the core: hard bands. Cost not measured.
- Did not: flutes of 0.2 m, 10 cm deep, on the bank line: a comb of teeth on the wall. Cost not measured.
- Did not: `npm run measure` hung for 8 minutes: time the still with the part hidden instead.
- Trap: shader errors show only at render: grep the output for ERROR (a name such as rim may be taken already).
- Trap: after merging main the crust may have moved off the brim: render main alone before blaming your change.
- (D3) Worked: changing cover and width with the surge (not kelvin). STATED, not measured with the cost tool: about 8 noise reads a lake pixel in the crust skin and 2 in the lava mesh; a 12-frame preview sheet took about 115 s before and after, so no cost seen.
- (D3) Worked: a sparse far field by large lanes times a break field, on the drawn surface only. No added cost seen (STATED).
- (D3) Worked: a table of vents with one dominant. No added cost (same instances) (STATED).
- (D3) Worked a little: the feet (six yellow knots of unlike size along the line at heart-0 in hd, the largest under the main fountain; small at the size of the whole frame; the seam between them still orange and even). No added instances (STATED).
- (D3) Did not: kelvin alone. It is lost in clipped orange and under the cover floor; the sheet's neighbour difference went from 3.81 to 3.94 and nothing was seen. Cost not measured.
- (D3) Did not: creep at 6 m and 2.3 m. It is under a pixel from the rim. Cost not measured.
- (D3) Did not: feet at 1 to 2.5 m (2 to 3 pixels at hd). Cost not measured.
- (D3) Did not: slab albedo (0.42 to 0.09) against the pale grey-blue ellipse left of the fountain seam at heart-0. D3 says the cause was not found and that it may not be the slab at all; D4, the later hand-back, found the cause (the next lines), and D4 holds.
- (D3) For scale, the predecessor's figure: the lava parts are 0.2 s of 72.5 s a sample at uhd draft. Not repeated (STATED).
- (D4) Worked: finding a stray pale shape by rendering each part alone (`--diag solo --part`). The pale grey-blue ellipse left of the fountain seam at heart-0 was the top of the foundering plate, level about (73, 72, 79) alone. Cause: the sky mirror in `rigShade`. Under 2 degrees the fresnel term is about 0.36 even at roughness 0.75. Cost not measured (one line of shade changed).
- (D4) Did not: lowering the plate's albedo (0.42 to 0.09) against a mirror of the sky: the mirror does not read the albedo. Cost not measured.
- Trap (D4): any flat, smooth, level face seen under 2 degrees mirrors the sky through the rig's fresnel term whatever its albedo or roughness; shade it along its normal and fully rough, or it reads as a pale lens.
- Still wrong (D3): (1) the fountain is still sparks over a flat seam with small knots; the fume above it is not lit by it, and the pale ellipse sits beside it (the pale ellipse was cured later, in D4). (2) The melt at the line is one flat level with painted plates; no thickness, no edge. (3) The surge is brightness changing in place, not flow; the near seams of the wide view hardly change at sheet size.
- Still wrong (D3), not done: thickness at the line; cost by the cost tool; plate turn and jostle at heart-03 never judged on a held sheet; the halo on the crust round the feeding pools does not pulse with the pools; the feet were looked at only at heart-0.

**The crust's form**

- Worked: central-difference normals from the bake: removed lit and unlit alternate vertices on edges askew to the lattice. Cost not measured.
- Worked: a `--diag depth` still to tell teeth in the height from teeth in the paint: 72 s a frame at uhd draft (MEASURED).
